# Canonical seed list for the matching-engine benchmark.
#
# Every scenario is generated deterministically from (scenario, count, seed).
# The published correctness hashes (reference/correctness_hash.txt) are for
# 1,000,000 new orders at seed 23 — the canonical seed. The seed is a
# calibration choice: the workload model is the paper benchmark's, and how
# deep a standing book a run carries is a property of the seed's price-path
# realisation (see docs/METHODOLOGY.md, "The standing book"). Seed 23 is
# published because each scenario's realisation carries a resting book
# representative of the regime the paper's benchmark runs exercised.
#
# seed   scenario      role
23       normal        canonical (published correctness hash)
23       static
23       swing-25
23       swing-40
23       flash-crash
#
# Consensus seeds (added 2026-10-15). 10,000 further seeds, drawn uniformly at random from [1, 2^32 - 1] with the
# operating system's cryptographic random source (23 excluded). For each seed, all five scenarios at 1,000,000 new
# orders have a hash in reference/correctness_hash.txt on which geseq/cpp-orderbook, LC++ v3 and CppTrader agree
# byte for byte, so `./harness --engine <your.so> --scenario <s> --seed <seed>` reports a Status of PASS or FAIL on
# them too. Exceptions are marked in the role column below. On 4 cells the LC++ v3 commit this repository pins
# (additional_references/lcv3_adapter) aborts: those workloads need more than the 32,766 live price levels its
# fixed-size level pool holds. LC++ v3 with that pool made growable (a one-line change) reproduces those hashes.
# scripts/validate_seeds.py runs an engine over all of these seeds, or over N of them drawn at random, and exits
# non-zero on any failure (for CI). These seeds are for self-testing beyond seed 23. An adversarial evaluation's
# held-out seed (docs/ANTI_CHEAT.md) must be one that is NOT in this list.
#
# seed        scenarios     role
